        ];

        // Retrieve the last shown message index from localStorage
        let currentIndex = parseInt(localStorage.getItem('currentMessageIndex') || '0', 10);

        // Hide all messages initially
        messages.forEach((message) => {
            message.style.display = 'none';
        });

        // Show the current message
        messages[currentIndex].style.display = 'flex';

        // Show the announcement bar
        setTimeout(() => {
            announcementBar.style.display = 'flex';
            document.body.classList.add('with-announcement');
        }, 10000); // 10-second delay

        // Update the index for the next page load
        currentIndex = (currentIndex + 1) % messages.length;
        localStorage.setItem('currentMessageIndex', currentIndex);

        // Close button functionality
        document.getElementById('close-announcement').addEventListener('click', function () {
            announcementBar.style.display = 'none';
            document.body.classList.remove('with-announcement');
        });
    });
</script>

				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-f91b226 elementor-widget elementor-widget-heading" data-id="f91b226" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-widget_type="heading.default">
					<h1 class="elementor-heading-title elementor-size-default">Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT</h1>				</div>
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-1fcc1d6c elementor-widget elementor-widget-theme-post-excerpt" data-id="1fcc1d6c" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-widget_type="theme-post-excerpt.default">
					Cyble Research Labs analyzes Borat , a sophisticated RAT variant that boasts a combination of Remote Access Trojan, Spyware, Ransomware and DDoS capabilities. 				</div>
				<div class="elementor-element elementor-element-2907e1e2 blog_content elementor-widget elementor-widget-theme-post-content" data-id="2907e1e2" data-element_type="widget" data-e-type="widget" data-widget_type="theme-post-content.default">
					
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Remote Access Trojan Capable of Conducting Ransomware &amp; DDOS Activities</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"></p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img fetchpriority="high" fetchpriority="high" decoding="async" width="790" height="387" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borat.png" alt="Remote Access Trojan Capable of Conducting Ransomware &amp; DDOS Activities" class="wp-image-10345" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 1" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borat.png 790w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borat-600x294.png 600w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borat-300x147.png 300w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borat-768x376.png 768w" sizes="(max-width: 790px) 100vw, 790px" /></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A Remote Access Trojan or RAT is a tool used by <a href="https://cyble.com/knowledge-hub/cyber-threat-actor-and-types/">Threat Actors </a>(TAs) to gain full access and remote control on a user&#8217;s system, including mouse and keyboard control, files access, and network resources access.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">During our regular OSINT research, Cyble Research Labs came across a new Remote Access Trojan (RAT) named <em>Borat</em>. Unlike other RATs, the Borat provides Ransomware, DDOS services, etc., to Threat Actors along with usual RAT features, further expanding the malware capabilities.</p><!-- This site is converting visitors into subscribers and customers with OptinMonster - https://api.omwpapi.com :: Campaign Title: Request demo blog -->
<div id="om-wlravxmwms40sunr2q0v-holder"></div>
<script>(function(d,u,ac){var s=d.createElement('script');s.type='text/javascript';s.src='https://a.omappapi.com/app/js/api.min.js';s.async=true;s.dataset.user=u;s.dataset.campaign=ac;d.getElementsByTagName('head')[0].appendChild(s);})(document,239265,'wlravxmwms40sunr2q0v');</script>
<!-- / OptinMonster -->



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The developer named this RAT &#8216;Borat&#8217; after a black comedy mockumentary film, and the photo used in the RAT is of actor Sacha Baron Cohen, who played the main role in the film Borat. The features claimed by the Borat RAT author are given in Figures 1 &amp; 2 below.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img decoding="async" width="790" height="474" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-1-List-of-Features-provided-by-the-Borat-RAT.png" alt="List of Features provided by the Borat RAT" class="wp-image-10346" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 2" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-1-List-of-Features-provided-by-the-Borat-RAT.png 790w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-1-List-of-Features-provided-by-the-Borat-RAT-600x360.png 600w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-1-List-of-Features-provided-by-the-Borat-RAT-300x180.png 300w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-1-List-of-Features-provided-by-the-Borat-RAT-768x461.png 768w" sizes="(max-width: 790px) 100vw, 790px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 1: List of Features provided by the Borat RAT</figcaption></figure>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img decoding="async" width="790" height="470" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-2-Another-List-of-Features-provided-by-Borat-RAT.png" alt="Additional Features provided by Borat RAT" class="wp-image-10349" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 3" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-2-Another-List-of-Features-provided-by-Borat-RAT.png 790w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-2-Another-List-of-Features-provided-by-Borat-RAT-600x357.png 600w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-2-Another-List-of-Features-provided-by-Borat-RAT-300x178.png 300w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-2-Another-List-of-Features-provided-by-Borat-RAT-768x457.png 768w" sizes="(max-width: 790px) 100vw, 790px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 2: Additional Features provided by Borat RAT</figcaption></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The Borat RAT provides a dashboard to Threat Actors (TAs) to perform RAT activities and also has an option to compile the <a href="https://cyble.com/knowledge-hub/what-is-malware/">malware</a> binary for performing DDoS and ransomware attacks on the victim&#8217;s machine, as shown in Figure 3.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="790" height="453" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-3-Borat-RAT-Dashboard.png" alt="Borat RAT Dashboard" class="wp-image-10350" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 4" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-3-Borat-RAT-Dashboard.png 790w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-3-Borat-RAT-Dashboard-600x344.png 600w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-3-Borat-RAT-Dashboard-300x172.png 300w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-3-Borat-RAT-Dashboard-768x440.png 768w" sizes="(max-width: 790px) 100vw, 790px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 3: Borat RAT Dashboard</figcaption></figure>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-technical-analysis">Technical Analysis</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In this analysis, we will take a look at Borat RAT and its features in detail. The Borat RAT comes as a package which includes builder binary, supporting modules, server certificate, etc., as shown in Figure 4.</p><!-- This site is converting visitors into subscribers and customers with OptinMonster - https://api.omwpapi.com :: Campaign Title: Cyble Blogs -->
<div id="om-r0hediyvvmvme9sqc9m4-holder"></div>
<script>(function(d,u,ac){var s=d.createElement('script');s.type='text/javascript';s.src='https://a.omappapi.com/app/js/api.min.js';s.async=true;s.dataset.user=u;s.dataset.campaign=ac;d.getElementsByTagName('head')[0].appendChild(s);})(document,239265,'r0hediyvvmvme9sqc9m4');</script>
<!-- / OptinMonster -->



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="632" height="183" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-4-Supporting-Modules-of-Borat.png" alt="Borat Package" class="wp-image-10352" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 5" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-4-Supporting-Modules-of-Borat.png 632w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-4-Supporting-Modules-of-Borat-600x174.png 600w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-4-Supporting-Modules-of-Borat-300x87.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 632px) 100vw, 632px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 4: Borat Package</figcaption></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The figure below shows the supporting modules responsible for executing the RAT features, as shown in Figure 5.</p><div id="cyble-1835172992" class="cyble-content_6 cyble-entity-placement" style="margin-bottom: 20px;"><a href="https://cyble.com/resources/research-reports/" aria-label="report-ad-banner"><img loading="lazy" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/report-ad-banner.webp" alt="report-ad-banner"  srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/report-ad-banner.webp 1200w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/report-ad-banner-300x45.webp 300w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/report-ad-banner-1024x152.webp 1024w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/report-ad-banner-768x114.webp 768w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/report-ad-banner-600x89.webp 600w" sizes="(max-width: 1200px) 100vw, 1200px" width="1200" height="178"   /></a></div>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="632" height="501" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-5-DLLs-used-to-Execute-all-Features.png" alt="DLLs used to execute Features" class="wp-image-10353" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 6" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-5-DLLs-used-to-Execute-all-Features.png 632w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-5-DLLs-used-to-Execute-all-Features-600x476.png 600w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-5-DLLs-used-to-Execute-all-Features-300x238.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 632px) 100vw, 632px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 5: DLLs used to execute Features</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Keylogger</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The module &#8220;<em>keylogger.exe</em>&#8221; is responsible for monitoring and storing the keystrokes in the victim&#8217;s machine. The below image shows the keyboard-related APIs used by the RAT for keylogging purposes. The captured keystrokes are saved in a file called &#8220;<em>Sa8XOfH1BudXLog.txt</em>&#8221; for exfiltration.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="183" height="242" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-6-Keyboard-APIs.png" alt="Figure 6 Keyboard APIs" class="wp-image-10354" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 7"><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 6: Keyboard APIs</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Ransomware</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Interestingly, the RAT has an option to deliver a ransomware payload to the victim&#8217;s machine for encrypting users&#8217; files as well as for demanding a ransom. Like other ransomware, this RAT also has the capability to create a ransom note on the victim&#8217;s machine.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="585" height="218" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-7-Ransomware-Note.png" alt="Figure 7 Ransomware Note" class="wp-image-10355" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 8" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-7-Ransomware-Note.png 585w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-7-Ransomware-Note-300x112.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 585px) 100vw, 585px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 7: Code to generate Ransom Note</figcaption></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The RAT has the code to decrypt files in the victim&#8217;s machine once the ransom is paid – as shown below.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="632" height="193" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-8-Decryption-Method.png" alt="Figure 8 Decryption Method" class="wp-image-10356" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 9" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-8-Decryption-Method.png 632w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-8-Decryption-Method-600x183.png 600w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-8-Decryption-Method-300x92.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 632px) 100vw, 632px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 8: Decryption Method</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-ddos">DDOS</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This RAT can also disrupt the normal traffic of a targeted server by performing a DDOS attack. The below figure shows the code used by RAT for the DDOS attack.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="433" height="212" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-9-Code-for-DDoS-Attack.png" alt="Code for DDoS Attack" class="wp-image-10357" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 10" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-9-Code-for-DDoS-Attack.png 433w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-9-Code-for-DDoS-Attack-300x147.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 433px) 100vw, 433px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 9: Code for DDoS Attack</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-audio-recording">Audio Recording</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Borat RAT can record the audio of a computer. Initially, it checks if a microphone is present in the victim&#8217;s machine. If it can find a connected microphone, the RAT records all audio and saves it in a file named <em>micaudio.wav</em>.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="362" height="262" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-10-Code-for-Mic-Recording.png" alt="Code for Mic Recording" class="wp-image-10358" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 11" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-10-Code-for-Mic-Recording.png 362w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-10-Code-for-Mic-Recording-300x217.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 362px) 100vw, 362px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 10: Code for Mic Recording</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-webcam-recording">Webcam Recording</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Borat RAT can capture videos through any webcam present in the victim&#8217;s machine. First, it identifies if a webcam is present in the victim&#8217;s machine, and then it starts recording the video if a webcam is available. &nbsp;</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="349" height="249" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-11-Webcam-Recording.png" alt="Webcam Recording" class="wp-image-10359" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 12" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-11-Webcam-Recording.png 349w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-11-Webcam-Recording-300x214.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 349px) 100vw, 349px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 11: Webcam Recording</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-remote-desktop">Remote Desktop</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This malware takes the remote desktop of the infected machine. It then gives the <a href="/blog/exposed-rdp-actively-targeted-by-threat-actors-to-deploy-ransomware/" title="Exposed Remote Desktop Protocol actively targeted by Threat Actors to deploy Ransomware" target="_blank" rel="noopener"  data-wpil-monitor-id="13022">Threat Actor</a> (TA) the necessary rights to perform activities such as controlling the victim&#8217;s machine, mouse, keyboard, and capturing the screen. Controlling the victim&#8217;s machine can allow TAs to perform several activities such as deleting critical files, executing ransomware in the compromised machine, etc. The below figure shows the functions used by the RAT for performing Remote Desktop activities.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full is-resized"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="155" height="121" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-12-Functions-used-for-Taking-Remote-Desktop.png" alt="Functions used for performing Remote Desktop Activities" class="wp-image-10361" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 13"><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 12: Functions used for performing Remote Desktop Activities</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-reverse-proxy">Reverse Proxy</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The RAT has code to enable reverse proxy for performing RAT activities anonymously. The TAs can hide their identity using this option while communicating with the compromised servers.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="370" height="199" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-13-Reverse-Proxy-Code.png" alt="Reverse Proxy Code" class="wp-image-10362" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 14" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-13-Reverse-Proxy-Code.png 370w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-13-Reverse-Proxy-Code-300x161.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 370px) 100vw, 370px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 13: Reverse Proxy Code</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-device-information">Device Information</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The RAT collects information from the victim&#8217;s machine, including OS Name, OS Version, System Model, etc. The below figure shows the command used for collecting the information.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="676" height="123" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-14-Command-used-for-Capturing-User-Info.png" alt="Command used for Capturing Device Info" class="wp-image-10364" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 15" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-14-Command-used-for-Capturing-User-Info.png 676w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-14-Command-used-for-Capturing-User-Info-600x109.png 600w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-14-Command-used-for-Capturing-User-Info-300x55.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 676px) 100vw, 676px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 14: Command used for Capturing Device Info</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Process hollowing</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Using the RAT, the TAs can inject malicious code into the legitimate processes using the process hollowing technique. The below figure shows the APIs used by the RAT for process hollowing.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="253" height="215" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-15-Process-Hollowing.png" alt="Process Hollowing" class="wp-image-10365" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 16"><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 15: Process Hollowing</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-browser-credential-stealing">Browser Credential Stealing</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Borat RAT can steal cookies, history, bookmarks, saved login credentials from chromium-based browsers like <a href="/blog/google-chrome-vulnerabilities-for-desktop-update/" title="Multiple Vulnerabilities in Google Chrome for Desktop: Update to Stay Secure" target="_blank" rel="noopener"  data-wpil-monitor-id="13024">Google Chrome</a>, Edge, etc.<strong> </strong>The below figure shows the functions used by the RAT for stealing browser credentials.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-full"><img loading="lazy" loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="323" height="248" src="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-16-Functions-used-for-Stealing-Browser-Credentials.png" alt="Functions used for Stealing Browser Credentials" class="wp-image-10366" title="Deep Dive Analysis – Borat RAT 17" srcset="https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-16-Functions-used-for-Stealing-Browser-Credentials.png 323w, https://cyble.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Figure-16-Functions-used-for-Stealing-Browser-Credentials-300x230.png 300w" sizes="(max-width: 323px) 100vw, 323px" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Figure 16: Functions used for Stealing Browser Credentials</figcaption></figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-discord-token-stealing">Discord token Stealing:</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">&nbsp;The RAT also steals Discord tokens and sends the stolen token information to the attacker.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-remote-activities">Remote Activities:</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The RAT performs the following activities to disturb the victims: Play Audio, Swap Mouse Buttons, Show/hide the Desktop, Show/hide the taskbar, Hold Mouse, Enable/Disable webcam light, Hang System, Monitor Off, Blank screen, etc.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="h-conclusion">Conclusion&nbsp;&nbsp;</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The Borat RAT is a potent and unique combination of Remote Access Trojan, Spyware, and <a href="https://cyble.com/knowledge-hub/what-is-ransomware/">Ransomware</a>, making it a triple threat to any machine compromised by it. With the capability to record audio and control the webcam and conduct traditional info stealing behavior, Borat is clearly a threat to keep an eye on. The added functionality to carry out <a href="/blog/new-mdbotnet-unleashes-ddos-attacks/" title="New MDBotnet Unleashes DDoS Attacks &#8211; Cyble" target="_blank" rel="noopener"  data-wpil-monitor-id="13020">DDOS attacks</a> makes this an even more dangerous threat that organizations and individuals need to look out for. The <a href="/blog/cyble-research-labs-q4-2021-ransomware-report/" title="Cyble Research Labs &#8211; Q4 2021 Ransomware Report" target="_blank" rel="noopener"  data-wpil-monitor-id="13026">Cyble Research</a> Team is closely monitoring the RAT&#8217;s actions and will keep informing our clients and people worldwide.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Our Recommendations &nbsp;</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">​We have listed some essential <a href="https://cyble.com/knowledge-hub/cybersecurity/">cybersecurity</a> best practices that create the first line of control against attackers. We recommend that our readers follow the suggestions given below: &nbsp;</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li>​Don&#8217;t keep important files in common locations such as the Desktop, My Documents, etc.&nbsp;</li>



<li>Use strong passwords and enforce multi-factor authentication wherever possible. &nbsp;</li>



<li>​Turn on the automatic software update feature on your computer, mobile, and other connected devices wherever possible and pragmatic.  &nbsp;</li>



<li>​Use a reputed anti-virus and <a href="/blog/the-rise-of-amadey-bot-a-growing-concern-for-internet-security/" title="The Rise of Amadey Bot: A Growing Concern for Internet Security" target="_blank" rel="noopener"  data-wpil-monitor-id="13028">Internet security</a> software package on your connected devices, including PC, laptop, and mobile.     &nbsp;</li>



<li>​Refrain from opening untrusted links and email attachments without verifying their authenticity. &nbsp;</li>



<li>Conduct regular backup practices and keep those backups offline or in a separate network. &nbsp;</li>
</ul>

<script>
   let autoHideTimeout;

document.addEventListener('scroll', function() {
    var scrollPosition = window.scrollY || document.documentElement.scrollTop;
    var documentHeight = document.documentElement.scrollHeight - document.documentElement.clientHeight;

    // Calculate scroll percentage
    var scrollPercentage = (scrollPosition / documentHeight) * 100;

    // Check if the screen width is greater than 768px (for non-mobile devices)
    if (window.innerWidth > 768) {
        // Only activate the slider when scroll is between 60% and 100%
        if (scrollPercentage >= 60 && scrollPercentage <= 100) {
            // Show the slider by sliding it in
            document.getElementById('rightSlider').classList.add('active');

            // Clear any previous auto-hide timer
            clearTimeout(autoHideTimeout);

            // Set a 5-second timer to auto-slide the slider back out
            autoHideTimeout = setTimeout(function() {
                document.getElementById('rightSlider').classList.remove('active');
            }, 5000); // 5000 milliseconds = 5 seconds
        } else {
            // If not within 60% - 100% range, hide the slider
            document.getElementById('rightSlider').classList.remove('active');
        }
    }
});

document.getElementById('rightSlider').addEventListener('mouseover', function() {
    // On mouseover, keep the slider active and clear the auto-hide timer
    clearTimeout(autoHideTimeout);
    this.classList.add('active');
});

document.getElementById('rightSlider').addEventListener('mouseleave', function() {
    // On mouse leave, start the auto-hide timer again for 5 seconds
    clearTimeout(autoHideTimeout);
    autoHideTimeout = setTimeout(function() {
        document.getElementById('rightSlider').classList.remove('active');
    }, 5000); // 5000 milliseconds = 5 seconds
});

</script>				</div>

				(function() {
					// Global page view and session tracking for UAEL Modal Popup feature
					try {
						// Session tracking: increment if this is a new session
						
						// Check if any popup on this page uses current page tracking
						var hasCurrentPageTracking = false;
						var currentPagePopups = [];
						// Check all modal popups on this page for current page tracking
						if (typeof jQuery !== 'undefined') {
							jQuery('.uael-modal-parent-wrapper').each(function() {
								var scope = jQuery(this).data('page-views-scope');
								var enabled = jQuery(this).data('page-views-enabled');
								var popupId = jQuery(this).attr('id').replace('-overlay', '');	
								if (enabled === 'yes' && scope === 'current') {
									hasCurrentPageTracking = true;
									currentPagePopups.push(popupId);
								}
							});
						}
						// Global tracking: ALWAYS increment if ANY popup on the site uses global tracking
												// Current page tracking: increment per-page counters
						if (hasCurrentPageTracking && currentPagePopups.length > 0) {
							var currentUrl = window.location.href;
							var urlKey = 'uael_page_views_' + btoa(currentUrl).replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9]/g, '').substring(0, 50);
							var currentPageViews = parseInt(localStorage.getItem(urlKey) || '0');
							currentPageViews++;
							localStorage.setItem(urlKey, currentPageViews.toString());
							// Store URL mapping for each popup
							for (var i = 0; i < currentPagePopups.length; i++) {
								var popupUrlKey = 'uael_popup_' + currentPagePopups[i] + '_url_key';
								localStorage.setItem(popupUrlKey, urlKey);
							}
						}
					} catch (e) {
						// Silently fail if localStorage is not available
					}
				})();
				</script>

{"@context":"https:\/\/schema.org\/","@type":"NewsArticle","@id":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/blog\/deep-dive-analysis-borat-rat\/#newsarticle","url":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/blog\/deep-dive-analysis-borat-rat\/","headline":"Deep Dive Analysis \u2013 Borat RAT | Cyble","mainEntityOfPage":"https:\/\/cyble.com\/blog\/deep-dive-analysis-borat-rat\/","datePublished":"2022-03-31T11:08:16-04:00","dateModified":"2025-05-20T05:10:00-04:00","description":"Cyble Research Labs analyzes Borat , a sophisticated RAT variant that boasts a combination of Remote Access Trojan, Spyware, Ransomware and DDoS capabilities.","articleSection":"Trojan","articleBody":"Remote Access Trojan Capable of Conducting Ransomware &amp; DDOS Activities            A Remote Access Trojan or RAT is a tool used by Threat Actors (TAs) to gain full access and remote control on a user's system, including mouse and keyboard control, files access, and network resources access.    During our regular OSINT research, Cyble Research Labs came across a new Remote Access Trojan (RAT) named Borat. Unlike other RATs, the Borat provides Ransomware, DDOS services, etc., to Threat Actors along with usual RAT features, further expanding the malware capabilities.    The developer named this RAT 'Borat' after a black comedy mockumentary film, and the photo used in the RAT is of actor Sacha Baron Cohen, who played the main role in the film Borat. The features claimed by the Borat RAT author are given in Figures 1 &amp; 2 below.    Figure 1: List of Features provided by the Borat RAT    Figure 2: Additional Features provided by Borat RAT    The Borat RAT provides a dashboard to Threat Actors (TAs) to perform RAT activities and also has an option to compile the malware binary for performing DDoS and ransomware attacks on the victim's machine, as shown in Figure 3.    Figure 3: Borat RAT Dashboard    Technical Analysis    In this analysis, we will take a look at Borat RAT and its features in detail. The Borat RAT comes as a package which includes builder binary, supporting modules, server certificate, etc., as shown in Figure 4.    Figure 4: Borat Package    The figure below shows the supporting modules responsible for executing the RAT features, as shown in Figure 5.    Figure 5: DLLs used to execute Features    Keylogger    The module \"keylogger.exe\" is responsible for monitoring and storing the keystrokes in the victim's machine. The below image shows the keyboard-related APIs used by the RAT for keylogging purposes. The captured keystrokes are saved in a file called \"Sa8XOfH1BudXLog.txt\" for exfiltration.    Figure 6: Keyboard APIs    Ransomware    Interestingly, the RAT has an option to deliver a ransomware payload to the victim's machine for encrypting users' files as well as for demanding a ransom. Like other ransomware, this RAT also has the capability to create a ransom note on the victim's machine.    Figure 7: Code to generate Ransom Note    The RAT has the code to decrypt files in the victim's machine once the ransom is paid \u2013 as shown below.    Figure 8: Decryption Method    DDOS    This RAT can also disrupt the normal traffic of a targeted server by performing a DDOS attack. The below figure shows the code used by RAT for the DDOS attack.    Figure 9: Code for DDoS Attack    Audio Recording    Borat RAT can record the audio of a computer. Initially, it checks if a microphone is present in the victim's machine. If it can find a connected microphone, the RAT records all audio and saves it in a file named micaudio.wav.    Figure 10: Code for Mic Recording    Webcam Recording    Borat RAT can capture videos through any webcam present in the victim's machine. First, it identifies if a webcam is present in the victim's machine, and then it starts recording the video if a webcam is available. &nbsp;    Figure 11: Webcam Recording    Remote Desktop    This malware takes the remote desktop of the infected machine. It then gives the Threat Actor (TA) the necessary rights to perform activities such as controlling the victim's machine, mouse, keyboard, and capturing the screen. Controlling the victim's machine can allow TAs to perform several activities such as deleting critical files, executing ransomware in the compromised machine, etc. The below figure shows the functions used by the RAT for performing Remote Desktop activities.    Figure 12: Functions used for performing Remote Desktop Activities    Reverse Proxy    The RAT has code to enable reverse proxy for performing RAT activities anonymously. The TAs can hide their identity using this option while communicating with the compromised servers.    Figure 13: Reverse Proxy Code    Device Information    The RAT collects information from the victim's machine, including OS Name, OS Version, System Model, etc. The below figure shows the command used for collecting the information.    Figure 14: Command used for Capturing Device Info    Process hollowing    Using the RAT, the TAs can inject malicious code into the legitimate processes using the process hollowing technique. The below figure shows the APIs used by the RAT for process hollowing.    Figure 15: Process Hollowing    Browser Credential Stealing    Borat RAT can steal cookies, history, bookmarks, saved login credentials from chromium-based browsers like Google Chrome, Edge, etc. The below figure shows the functions used by the RAT for stealing browser credentials.    Figure 16: Functions used for Stealing Browser Credentials    Discord token Stealing:    &nbsp;The RAT also steals Discord tokens and sends the stolen token information to the attacker.    Remote Activities:    The RAT performs the following activities to disturb the victims: Play Audio, Swap Mouse Buttons, Show\/hide the Desktop, Show\/hide the taskbar, Hold Mouse, Enable\/Disable webcam light, Hang System, Monitor Off, Blank screen, etc.&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;    Conclusion&nbsp;&nbsp;    The Borat RAT is a potent and unique combination of Remote Access Trojan, Spyware, and Ransomware, making it a triple threat to any machine compromised by it. With the capability to record audio and control the webcam and conduct traditional info stealing behavior, Borat is clearly a threat to keep an eye on. The added functionality to carry out DDOS attacks makes this an even more dangerous threat that organizations and individuals need to look out for. The Cyble Research Team is closely monitoring the RAT's actions and will keep informing our clients and people worldwide.    Our Recommendations\u202f&nbsp;    \u200bWe have listed some essential cybersecurity best practices that create the first line of control against attackers. We recommend that our readers follow the suggestions given below:\u202f&nbsp;     \u200bDon't keep important files in common locations such as the Desktop, My Documents, etc.&nbsp;    Use strong passwords and enforce multi-factor authentication wherever possible.\u202f&nbsp;    \u200bTurn on the automatic software update feature on your computer, mobile, and other connected devices wherever possible and pragmatic.\u202f\u202f&nbsp;    \u200bUse a reputed anti-virus and Internet security software package on your connected devices, including PC, laptop, and mobile.\u202f\u202f\u202f\u202f\u202f&nbsp;    \u200bRefrain from opening untrusted links and email attachments without verifying their authenticity.\u202f&nbsp;    Conduct regular backup practices and keep those backups offline or in a separate network.\u202f&nbsp;     MITRE ATT&amp;CK\u00ae Techniques&nbsp;    \u200bTactic&nbsp;\u200bTechnique ID&nbsp;\u200bTechnique 					const lazyloadBackgroundObserver = new IntersectionObserver( ( entries ) => {

								if( lazyloadBackground ) {
									lazyloadBackground.classList.add( 'e-lazyloaded' );
								}

						lazyloadBackgroundObserver.observe( lazyloadBackground );

ZFAdvLead.prototype.zfautm_sC = function( paramName,path,domain,secure ){
  var value = ZFAdvLead.utmPValObj[paramName];
  if ( typeof value !== "undefined" && value !== null ){
    var cookieStr = paramName + "=" + encodeURIComponent( value );
    var exdate=new Date();
    exdate.setDate(exdate.getDate()+7);
    cookieStr += "; expires=" + exdate.toGMTString();
    cookieStr += "; path=/";
    if ( domain ) {
      cookieStr += "; domain=" + encodeURIComponent( domain );
    }
    if ( secure ) {
      cookieStr += "; secure";
    }
    document.cookie = cookieStr;
  }

  }

ZFAdvLead.prototype.zfautm_calcTrafSrc = function() {

    u3 = this.zfautm_gP(url_search, 'utm_campaign');

    u5 = this.zfautm_gP(url_search, 'utm_content');

    var r_u5 = this.zfautm_gC('utm_content');

          ref_path = ref_path.substr(0, ref_path.indexOf('?'));

      }

      }

  } else {

ZFAdvLead.prototype.zfautm_gP = function(s, q) {
  try{
      var match = s.match('[?&]' + q + '=([^&]+)');
      if ( match ) {
        if ( match[1].length > 199 ) {
          var raw = decodeURIComponent(match[1]);
          raw = raw.replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9_]/g, '');
          return raw.slice( 0, 199 );
        } else {
          return decodeURIComponent(match[1]);
        }
      } else {
        return '';
      }
  } catch(e){
    return '';  
  }
}
ZFAdvLead.prototype.zfautm_gC = function( cookieName ){
  var cookieArr = document.cookie.split('; ');
  for ( var i = 0 ; i < cookieArr.length ; i ++ ){
    var cookieVals = cookieArr[i].split('=');
      if ( cookieVals[0] === cookieName && cookieVals[1] ) {
        return decodeURIComponent(cookieVals[1]);
      }
  }
};
ZFAdvLead.prototype.zfautm_gC_enc = function( cookieName ){
  var cookieArr = document.cookie.split('; ');
  for ( var i = 0 ; i < cookieArr.length ; i ++ ){
    var cookieVals = cookieArr[i].split('=');
      if ( cookieVals[0] === cookieName && cookieVals[1] ) {
        return cookieVals[1];
      }
  }
};
ZFAdvLead.prototype.zfautm_iframeSprt = function () {
  var zf_frame = document.getElementsByTagName("iframe");
  for(var i = 0; i < zf_frame.length; ++i){
    if((zf_frame[i].src).indexOf('formperma') > 0 ){
      var zf_src = zf_frame[i].src;
      for( var prmIdx = 0 ; prmIdx < ZFAdvLead.utmPNameArr.length ; prmIdx ++ ) {
        var utmPm = ZFAdvLead.utmPNameArr[ prmIdx ];
        utmPm = ( ZFAdvLead.isSameDomain && ( ZFAdvLead.utmcustPNameArr.indexOf(utmPm) == -1 ) ) ? "zf_" + utmPm : utmPm;
        var utmPmregex = new RegExp("[?&]" + utmPm + "=");
        if ( ! utmPmregex.test(zf_src) ) {
          var utmVal = this.zfautm_gC_enc( ZFAdvLead.utmPNameArr[ prmIdx ] );
          if ( typeof utmVal !== "undefined" ) {
            if ( utmVal != "" ){
              if(zf_src.indexOf('?') > 0){
                zf_src = zf_src+'&'+utmPm+'='+ utmVal;
              }else{
                zf_src = zf_src+'?'+utmPm+'='+ utmVal;
              }
            }
          }
        }
      }
      if ( zf_frame[i].src.length < zf_src.length ) {
        zf_frame[i].src = zf_src;
      }
    }
  }
};
ZFAdvLead.prototype.zfautm_DHtmlSprt = function () {

            if ( fieldObj ) {
              fieldObj.value = utmVal;
            }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
};
